VFQEC / RESEARCH EXTENSION

Can calibration use fewer probes?

Bounded Fourier coordinate calibration compared with budget-matched and reference SPSA. A model-specific exploratory study with a frozen held-out panel.

24 of 24 primary independent cases present. The planned held-out seed panel is complete. Noninferiority margin: 0.001 absolute probability, or 0.1 percentage points. Neither noninferiority nor zero observed errors establishes equality.

Fourier versus SPSA · 604k shots

0.0146 pp

Mean paired difference; 95% interval [-0.0115, 0.0396] pp. Negative favors Fourier.

noninferiority supported within the predeclared model-specific margin.

Secondary, evaluation-only exact probability difference: 0.0091 pp; 95% case-bootstrap interval [0.0065, 0.0120] pp. Exact probabilities do not select the controller.

Mean paired difference; 95% interval [-0.0208, 0.0281] pp. Negative favors Fourier.

Interpretation limits

  • This is a model-specific exploratory software study with held-out data, not a universal performance claim, hardware experiment, or fault-tolerant threshold measurement.
  • The primary endpoint is the observed final-round failure probability; exact probabilities are secondary evaluation-only diagnostics and are not used to select a controller.
  • The 10,000-repeat percentile bootstrap resamples paired independent cases, never correlated round endpoints or stabilizer bits. Its interval describes this sampled case distribution.
  • The predeclared noninferiority margin is 0.001 absolute failure probability (0.1 percentage points). This study-specific tolerance does not mean equal performance.
  • Equal-budget calibration is approximate: 60,000 versus 61,000 probe shots. All fitting and validation probes count toward each optimizer's reported budget.
  • A lower syndrome cost does not certify a lower logical error. The three-point sinusoidal coordinate identity applies to a one-round probe with one occurrence of each control.
  • State preparation, extraction, recovery, and terminal data readout are otherwise ideal; the decoder is spatial per round. Calibration uses fresh probes and freezes memory time.
  • Zero observed failures do not establish zero physical error. Extension groups and exact probability diagnostics are exploratory, without multiplicity-adjusted confirmation.
  • BlueQubit verification runs reuse local design cases and are not additional independent primary observations. The cloud backend supplies coherent simulation; noise, syndrome measurement, and recovery are local instrumentation.
  • This adapts known Rotosolve and syndrome-calibration ideas. Scientific novelty is not established by a successful benchmark or by this literature review.
  • The frozen drift runner executed three reference-SPSA calibrations but evaluated only its static reference arm. Later reference recalibrations were unused by that arm; their probe shots remain included in total executed calibration costs and in raw records. Static and adaptive arms can share calibrations, so do not sum arm costs as independent work.

Prior art and novelty boundary

  • Ostaszewski et al. — Structure optimization for parameterized quantum circuits. Sinusoidal coordinate minimization (Rotosolve) predates this software.
  • Wagner et al. — Optimal noise estimation from syndrome statistics. Noise estimation and identifiability from syndrome data are established.
  • Magann et al. — Fast-feedback protocols for calibration and drift control. Prior work includes simulated syndrome-only calibration during QEC.
  • Kunjummen and Taylor — In situ calibration during quantum error correction. Prior work combines decoder feedback and injected calibration operations.
  • Özgüler — Securing quantum error correction against misleading advice. Recovery validation requires attention to uncertainty, drift, and sign ambiguity.
  • Pan et al. — Identifying the Sign of Coherent Over-Rotations. Passive observations need not identify coherent signs; active probes can help.

  "limitations": [
    "This is a model-specific exploratory software study with held-out data, not a universal performance claim, hardware experiment, or fault-tolerant threshold measurement.",
    "The primary endpoint is the observed final-round failure probability; exact probabilities are secondary evaluation-only diagnostics and are not used to select a controller.",
    "The 10,000-repeat percentile bootstrap resamples paired independent cases, never correlated round endpoints or stabilizer bits. Its interval describes this sampled case distribution.",
    "The predeclared noninferiority margin is 0.001 absolute failure probability (0.1 percentage points). This study-specific tolerance does not mean equal performance.",
    "Equal-budget calibration is approximate: 60,000 versus 61,000 probe shots. All fitting and validation probes count toward each optimizer's reported budget.",
    "A lower syndrome cost does not certify a lower logical error. The three-point sinusoidal coordinate identity applies to a one-round probe with one occurrence of each control.",
    "State preparation, extraction, recovery, and terminal data readout are otherwise ideal; the decoder is spatial per round. Calibration uses fresh probes and freezes memory time.",
    "Zero observed failures do not establish zero physical error. Extension groups and exact probability diagnostics are exploratory, without multiplicity-adjusted confirmation.",
    "BlueQubit verification runs reuse local design cases and are not additional independent primary observations. The cloud backend supplies coherent simulation; noise, syndrome measurement, and recovery are local instrumentation.",
    "This adapts known Rotosolve and syndrome-calibration ideas. Scientific novelty is not established by a successful benchmark or by this literature review.",
    "The frozen drift runner executed three reference-SPSA calibrations but evaluated only its static reference arm. Later reference recalibrations were unused by that arm; their probe shots remain included in total executed calibration costs and in raw records. Static and adaptive arms can share calibrations, so do not sum arm costs as independent work."
